- **princi**: Retrieves context from Drive, Gmail, Slack, Calendar, and memory, then surfaces decisions and detailed action items. - **principle-assert-invariants-not-last-bug**: Apply after a bug ships past review or a passing grade/check: assert the general class of failure instead of patching only the specific instance that was caught. Use on /reflect, whenever a fix's own commit message reads like 'fix X for ticker/case Y', or whenever a fix makes a row disappear instead of shipping it with a status. - **principle-async-for-volume**: Apply to anything beyond small data volumes. Move volume out of the synchronous path: queueable for chainable jobs, batch Apex for large data volumes, Bulk API 2.0 for loads, platform events for decoupling. The sync path is for work the user is waiting on. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-async-for-volume))
- **principle-atomic-types**: Apply when writing or reviewing TypeScript application functions, module APIs, dependency injection, services, use cases, controllers, or domain logic. ([dakdevs/skills](https://github.com/dakdevs/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-atomic-types))
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. - **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when repeated fixes sharing an assumption fail. State the assumption and choose an observation that can challenge it before trying another fix that depends on it. ([michael-denyer/pstack-claude](https://github.com/michael-denyer/pstack-claude/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★497 · 2 copies)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Question the shared premise before the next fix. For imbalance problems, take a census per actor and test the asymmetry hypothesis. ([ericlitman/open-pstack](https://github.com/ericlitman/open-pstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★286 · 2 copies)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Take a census of which actors hold the imbalance before the next fix, then question the premise instead of writing another fix that assumes it. ([Aqua-123/pstack-for-codex](https://github.com/Aqua-123/pstack-for-codex/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★70 · 40 copies)

- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when repeated fixes fail and a shared assumption may be wrong. ([jaydubya818/skillz](https://github.com/jaydubya818/skillz/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★1)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: When two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same check, stop fixing, write the premise down, and test it before the next fix. - **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when repeated fixes rely on the same assumption and fail the same check. Test that assumption before trying another variation. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · 2 copies)

- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Inventory who actually owns or exhibits the imbalance, then challenge the shared premise before writing another patch. - **principle-avoid-tautological-tests**: Apply when writing or reviewing automated tests, regression tests, expected values, fixtures, mocks, snapshots, property tests, or coverage-driven test additions. Tautological tests are harmful: require an independent test oracle and a credible way for each test to fail under a plausible defect; reject assertions that merely restate production logic, fixture setup, mock configuration, or the value under test. ([dakdevs/skills](https://github.com/dakdevs/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-avoid-tautological-tests))
- **principle-bind-to-named-inventory**: Apply after an audit, scoring review, or a request for a "real classifier" / "less arbitrary weights" when existing scorers, rankers, or weight formulas were already named. - **principle-block-on-the-human**: Apply when an open question could be answered by the person in front of you. - **principle-bound-retries**: Make retries safe, classified, budgeted, and capacity-aware across every execution layer. - **principle-bounded-repair**: Bound every repair loop — retrying an identical failure the same way burns budget without generating information. Apply during any fix loop, CI-chasing, or repeated verification failure. ([vraj-ai/skills](https://github.com/vraj-ai/skills/tree/HEAD/principles/principle-bounded-repair) · ★4)
- **principle-build-the-lever**: Apply to any non-trivial work, not just bulk work: edits, migrations, analyses, checks. - **principle-minimum-necessary-complexity**: Apply when choosing a solution or evaluating additions to code, state, layers, configuration, and public surface area. Add only the complexity the observed outcome requires. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-minimum-necessary-complexity) · 2 copies)
- **principle-model-durable-work**: Give recoverable work durable identity, state, progress, attempts, and outcomes independent of its workers. Mandatory when an accepted business obligation must survive the request or process that accepted it, resume after worker loss, or move between workers. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-model-durable-work) · ★1)
- **principle-model-the-domain**: Apply when writing stateful logic, or when code branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-model-the-domain) · ★8.2k · 2.1k installs · 27 copies)
- **principle-model-the-domain**: Apply when writing stateful logic, or when code branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files. Encode the domain in a structure instead of scattered conditionals. ([michael-denyer/pstack-claude](https://github.com/michael-denyer/pstack-claude/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-model-the-domain) · ★497 · 73 copies)
- **principle-model-the-domain**: Apply when repeated state conditionals reveal a missing domain model. ([jaydubya818/skillz](https://github.com/jaydubya818/skillz/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-model-the-domain) · ★1)
- **principle-model-the-domain**: Replace scattered conditionals, synchronized booleans, repeated shape assumptions, and ad hoc mutations with a structure that directly represents the domain. ([3metaJun/mstack](https://github.com/3metaJun/mstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-model-the-domain))
- **principle-model-the-domain**: When you write stateful logic, or code branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files, encode the domain in a data structure instead of scattered conditionals. In existing code, find implicit state machines and make them explicit. ([kpeez/agent-toolbox](https://github.com/kpeez/agent-toolbox/tree/HEAD/plugins/swe/skills/principle-model-the-domain))
- **principle-model-the-domain**: Apply when writing stateful logic, or when code branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files. Encode the domain in a structure (a state machine, a typed model, a registry, the right collection) instead of scattered conditionals. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-model-the-domain))
- **principle-model-the-domain**: Apply when states, rules, transitions, or ownership are repeated across fields, branches, callers, or files. Give each domain fact one authoritative representation and derive the rest. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-model-the-domain) · 2 copies)
- **principle-model-the-domain**: Apply when writing stateful logic, a growing if/else chain, or a shape assumption repeated across files. ([MVPavan/coding-ritual](https://github.com/MVPavan/coding-ritual/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-model-the-domain))
- **principle-model-the-domain**: Encode the domain in the right structure: state machine, typed model, table, registry, reducer, boundary, or collection instead of scattered conditionals. Use when when stateful logic accumulates branches or repeated shape assumptions. ([TheOnlyFusionCube/potetos-for-everyone](https://github.com/TheOnlyFusionCube/potetos-for-everyone/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-model-the-domain))
- **principle-name-the-destination**: Apply before decomposing work into tickets, phases, or a map. State what done looks like before you break anything into parts. ([mattlindell/photon-plugins](https://github.com/mattlindell/photon-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/ph-plan/skills/principles/principle-name-the-destination))
- **principle-name-the-scorer**: Apply when a product has two (or more) scorers that both emit weights, priorities, or rankings — especially hand-authored theme/report priorities beside learned historical-case tag weights. Name which scorer produced which number; never imply one sets the other unless wiring is explicit. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-name-the-scorer) · ★2)
- **principle-named-constraints**: List and name every delivery constraint — budget, spend authority, git authority, tracker access, time, state trust — before starting work, because an unnamed constraint gets violated and a named one chooses the path. Apply before executing any task whose environment may restrict the obvious path. ([vraj-ai/skills](https://github.com/vraj-ai/skills/tree/HEAD/principles/principle-named-constraints) · ★4)
- **principle-naming**: Use when naming or renaming anything another reader will meet later: a file, a directory, a report or document, a scratch artifact, a variable, function, type, constant, branch, or commit subject. Fires before writing any new file, whenever a name is about to carry a generic stem, a number, a date, a version word, an acronym, or a codename, and when reviewing names in a diff. Exists to stop one failure: names that only decode for someone who was in the session. ([Uraxii/dotai](https://github.com/Uraxii/dotai/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-naming))
- **principle-never-block-on-the-human**: Apply when tempted to ask 'should I do X?' on reversible work. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-never-block-on-the-human) · ★8.2k · 2.2k installs · 26 copies)
- **principle-never-block-on-the-human**: Apply when tempted to ask 'should I do X?' on reversible work. Proceed, present the result, let the human course-correct after the fact; reserve confirmation for irreversible actions. ([michael-denyer/pstack-claude](https://github.com/michael-denyer/pstack-claude/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-never-block-on-the-human) · ★497 · 73 copies)
- **principle-never-block-on-the-human**: Apply when tempted to ask 'should I do X?' on reversible work. Proceed, present the result, let the human course-correct after the fact; stay within authorized scope and reserve clarification for real missing decisions. ([fullerzz/agent-skills](https://github.com/fullerzz/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-never-block-on-the-human) · ★2)
- **principle-never-block-on-the-human**: Apply when tempted to ask about a low-risk, reversible implementation detail inside the authorized scope. Proceed and present the result, but preserve product-owner decisions, approval policy, and external-write boundaries. ([jaydubya818/MissionControl](https://github.com/jaydubya818/MissionControl/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-never-block-on-the-human) · ★2)
- **principle-never-block-on-the-human**: Apply when safe reversible implementation details can proceed inside scope. ([jaydubya818/skillz](https://github.com/jaydubya818/skillz/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-never-block-on-the-human) · ★1)
- **principle-never-block-on-the-human**: When you want to ask "should I do X?" about reversible work, do X, present the result, and let the human course-correct afterward. ([kpeez/agent-toolbox](https://github.com/kpeez/agent-toolbox/tree/HEAD/plugins/swe/skills/principle-never-block-on-the-human))
- **principle-never-block-on-the-human**: Apply when execution can continue through reversible work. Infer intent, investigate facts, and produce a reviewable artifact without repeated permission. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-never-block-on-the-human) · 2 copies)
- **principle-never-block-on-the-human**: Proceed on facts and reversible preparation; stop for human-owned decisions. ([taekwondodev/skills](https://github.com/taekwondodev/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-never-block-on-the-human))
- **principle-never-block-on-the-human**: Run the experiment or make the reversible best-effort change, then show the result. Ask only for genuine preference, authority, or irreversible decisions. Use when when a reversible empirical or implementation choice can be resolved with available tools. ([TheOnlyFusionCube/potetos-for-everyone](https://github.com/TheOnlyFusionCube/potetos-for-everyone/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-never-block-on-the-human))
- **principle-no-hardcoded-ids**: Apply when reviewing Apex, Flow, formula, or integration config. No hard-coded record IDs, user IDs, profile IDs, record-type IDs, or org-specific URLs anywhere. IDs differ per org; references go through custom metadata types, custom labels, Custom Settings, or queries by name. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-no-hardcoded-ids))
- **principle-no-lookahead**: Apply to any evaluation, backtest, benchmark, grader, or training run over data with a time axis or an ordered decision sequence. Score a decision only against inputs that existed before that decision was made. Covers what the field calls look-ahead bias, target leakage, point-in-time correctness, and repainting. Reach for it on: a backtest or walk-forward run, a feature computed over a full history, a retrieval or agent eval, a label assigned after the outcome was known, or any result that looks too good. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-no-lookahead) · ★2)
- **principle-observability**: Observability principles — logs vs metrics vs traces, structured logging, distributed tracing, span/trace context, cardinality control, SLI/SLO/SLA, RED method, USE method, alerting on symptoms vs causes. Auto-load when adding logging, choosing a metric, instrumenting distributed tracing, defining SLIs/SLOs, designing alerts, or discussing observability budgets. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-observability) · ★2)
- **principle-observe-operations**: Establish operation ownership and one coherent completion observation when adding or reviewing instrumentation across logs, traces, or metrics. Use for execution boundaries and outcome meaning; pure calculations and ordinary getters do not require instrumentation. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-observe-operations) · ★1)
- **principle-observe-progress**: Define useful advancement, legitimate waits, and fresh live evidence when instrumenting or reviewing streams, long-running operations, or stall and spin reports. Does not change durable-work or recovery authority. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-observe-progress) · ★1)
- **principle-of-optimism**: Apply David Deutsch's Principle of Optimism, all evils are caused by insufficient knowledge, and all problems are soluble unless forbidden by the laws of physics. Use when the user is paralyzed by pessimism, defaultism, or "this is just how it is" resignation. Useful for product roadmaps, social problems, technical impossibilities, and existential framings. Sourced from "The Beginning of Infinity" by David Deutsch, Chapter 9. ([adamtpang/summon.guide](https://github.com/adamtpang/summon.guide/tree/HEAD/plugins/deutsch/skills/principle-of-optimism))
- **principle-one**: Fixture principle skill. ([Steamedhams89/hermes-pstack](https://github.com/Steamedhams89/hermes-pstack/tree/HEAD/tests/fixtures/parity/mini-source/skills/principle-one))
- **principle-one-way-doors-first**: Apply when sequencing decisions or ordering a plan. Surface and settle irreversible decisions early, while changing your mind is still cheap. ([mattlindell/photon-plugins](https://github.com/mattlindell/photon-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/ph-plan/skills/principles/principle-one-way-doors-first))
- **principle-operational-control**: Keep accepted and recovery work within finite capacity through explicit budgets, backpressure, admission, isolation, and observable objectives. Mandatory when changing acceptance, buffering, concurrency, retry amplification, rate or cost limits, tenant isolation, or overload behavior. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-operational-control) · ★1)
- **principle-outcome-oriented-execution**: Apply during planned rewrites and migrations with explicit phase boundaries. Converge on the target architecture; don't preserve smooth intermediate states with throwaway compatibility code. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-outcome-oriented-execution) · ★8.2k · 2.2k installs · 101 copies)
- **principle-outcome-oriented-execution**: Apply whenever a change threads a new required value through existing callers, not just named rewrites/migrations — a new required field, a new parameter, a new required IPC/API argument. Converge on the target shape in one slice; don't preserve smooth intermediate states with throwaway compatibility code. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-outcome-oriented-execution) · ★2)
- **principle-outcome-oriented-execution**: Apply during planned rewrites and migrations with explicit phase boundaries. Converge on the target architecture; don't preserve smooth intermediate states with throwaway compatibility code. Use this skill when the user asks for principle outcome oriented execution or the task clearly matches this workflow. ([jaydubya818/MissionControl](https://github.com/jaydubya818/MissionControl/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-outcome-oriented-execution) · ★2)
- **principle-outcome-oriented-execution**: Apply when a bounded rewrite should converge without throwaway compatibility layers. ([jaydubya818/skillz](https://github.com/jaydubya818/skillz/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-outcome-oriented-execution) · ★1)
- **principle-outcome-oriented-execution**: Apply during planned rewrites and migrations with explicit phase boundaries. Accept intermediate breakage only after planning and scoping it and confirming that you can reverse it. Move toward the target architecture without adding throwaway compatibility code to smooth those phases. ([hellsan631/skills](https://github.com/hellsan631/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principles/principle-outcome-oriented-execution))
- **principle-outcome-oriented-execution**: During a planned rewrite or migration with explicit phase boundaries, converge on the target architecture instead of keeping intermediate states smooth with throwaway compatibility code. ([kpeez/agent-toolbox](https://github.com/kpeez/agent-toolbox/tree/HEAD/plugins/swe/skills/principle-outcome-oriented-execution))
- **principle-outcome-oriented-execution**: Apply during planned rewrites and migrations with explicit phase boundaries. Converge on the target architecture; do not preserve smooth intermediate states with throwaway compatibility code. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-outcome-oriented-execution))
- **principle-outcome-oriented-execution**: Converge on the target architecture rather than preserving temporary compatibility states as permanent complexity. Use when during planned migrations or rewrites with explicit phases. ([TheOnlyFusionCube/potetos-for-everyone](https://github.com/TheOnlyFusionCube/potetos-for-everyone/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-outcome-oriented-execution))
- **principle-own-coherent-responsibilities**: Apply when you create or split a module, decide where state belongs, or consider extracting shared code. ([nilpath/nilpath-marketplace](https://github.com/nilpath/nilpath-marketplace/tree/HEAD/dist/claude/plugins/nstack/skills/principle-own-coherent-responsibilities) · 3 copies)
- **principle-performance**: Performance engineering principles — latency vs throughput, profile-before-optimize discipline, Big-O instincts for common patterns, data locality and cache-friendliness, N+1 queries on a list endpoint, TTL sizing for hot-path endpoints, stampede prevention with single-flight. Auto-load when reviewing hot-path code, choosing data structures, designing batch or streaming pipelines, hunting allocations or GC pauses, sizing TTL and picking cache invalidation, or evaluating scalability. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-performance) · ★2)
- **principle-postmortem**: Postmortem principles — blameless culture, root cause analysis (5 Whys, Fishbone), incident document structure, MTTD/MTTR metrics. Auto-load when running a blameless review, facilitating RCA, tracking action items after an outage, or improving incident response processes. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-postmortem) · ★2)
- **principle-prefer-pure-functional-patterns**: Prefer pure calculations and explicit effects when creating, changing, reviewing, or diagnosing transformations, validators, reducers, pipelines, or state changes. Preserve the domain's command and ownership contracts. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-prefer-pure-functional-patterns) · ★1)
- **principle-preserve-substitution-contracts**: Apply when you add or replace an implementation of an existing contract. Preserve the inputs, results, invariants, errors, and effects that callers rely on. ([nilpath/nilpath-marketplace](https://github.com/nilpath/nilpath-marketplace/tree/HEAD/dist/claude/plugins/nstack/skills/principle-preserve-substitution-contracts) · 3 copies)
- **principle-preserve-type-flow**: Apply when writing or reviewing TypeScript that introduces type annotations, `as` casts, validation, Zod schemas, controllers, API boundaries, type guards, assertion helpers, or duplicated domain types. Preserve type information inferred from authoritative upstream producers; reject `as Type` assertions that override compiler evidence while allowing `as const` to retain value-derived literal types; parse externally supplied raw data once at the entrypoint with Zod when no trusted typed source exists; avoid functions whose purpose is checking a value's TypeScript type. ([dakdevs/skills](https://github.com/dakdevs/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-preserve-type-flow))
- **principle-product-design**: UX and product design principles — Nielsen's 10 usability heuristics, visual hierarchy, interaction design patterns, design-system compliance, and responsive layout. Accessibility/WCAG conformance is delegated to `swe-workbench:principle-accessibility`. Auto-load when reviewing UX, evaluating usability, assessing visual hierarchy or information architecture, auditing design-system compliance, or planning user-facing features. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-product-design) · ★2)
- **principle-prove-it**: Apply before any claim about code, systems, or history. No claim without same-turn evidence; a hedge is a trigger to verify, never a place to stop. Routes the four investigation products that build the evidence. Unlike the other principles this one auto-fires: it must intercept a claim as it forms, not be named after the fact. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-prove-it) · ★2)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.' ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★8.2k · 2.6k installs · 104 copies)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply before declaring any task or fix done. A habit, not a generated gate: run the real artifact, read the actual value, inspect the diff. Never a self-report, proxy, or 'it compiles.' ([itsvedantkumar/vstack](https://github.com/itsvedantkumar/vstack/tree/HEAD/claude/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★5 · 3 copies)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.' Use this skill when the user asks for principle prove it works or the task clearly matches this workflow. ([jaydubya818/MissionControl](https://github.com/jaydubya818/MissionControl/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★2)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply when the actual candidate and observable result still need verification. ([jaydubya818/skillz](https://github.com/jaydubya818/skillz/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★1)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Verify completed work against the real artifact before declaring success. Use after implementation, configuration, migration, automation, or delegated work when compilation or self-report is not enough. ([3metaJun/mstack](https://github.com/3metaJun/mstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works))
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after completing a task and before declaring it done. Verify the real artifact by running the feature, reading the actual value, or inspecting the diff. Proxies, self-reports, and successful compilation do not prove the task works. ([hellsan631/skills](https://github.com/hellsan631/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principles/principle-prove-it-works))
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Before you claim that a task is done or a change works, check the real artifact through its real entry point and give a VERIFIED, NOT VERIFIED, or INCONCLUSIVE verdict. ([kpeez/agent-toolbox](https://github.com/kpeez/agent-toolbox/tree/HEAD/plugins/swe/skills/principle-prove-it-works))
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact in a real org (run the behavior, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, a self-report, or 'it compiles'. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works))
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply before declaring an artifact complete or accepting a subagent's work. Claim an outcome only with evidence that directly supports it. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · 2 copies)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Verify the requested behavior against the real artifact or surface whenever possible; a proxy check only proves the proxy. Use when verifying changes, completing tasks, or claiming something is fixed. ([TheOnlyFusionCube/potetos-for-everyone](https://github.com/TheOnlyFusionCube/potetos-for-everyone/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works))
- **principle-push-not-poll**: Apply when designing, reviewing, or authoring any mechanism that waits on a long-running background action: a CI/build watcher, a deploy or merge poller, a status-check loop, an agent babysitting a backgrounded command. Prefer a push/wakeup signal over a tight poll loop. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-push-not-poll) · ★2)
- **principle-read-state-artifacts**: Apply when designing, reviewing, or authoring any mechanism that checks on work a background process already tracks: a babysit/watcher loop, a status check on a queue or worker, an agent verifying what a cron already did. Prefer reading the state artifact the worker already wrote (a ledger, digest, or status file) over re-deriving that state with live commands inside a session transcript. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-read-state-artifacts) · ★2)
- **principle-real-artifact**: Verify every claimed outcome against the real artifact — run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff — never against a proxy, a self-report, or a build pass. Apply after completing any task, before declaring done, and whenever reading evidence produced by a worker, a ticket, or a pipeline state. ([vraj-ai/skills](https://github.com/vraj-ai/skills/tree/HEAD/principles/principle-real-artifact) · ★4)
- **principle-record-effective-inputs**: Expose or retain every input that can change a result, and match evidence to deterministic, stochastic, concurrent, or externally controlled behavior. Mandatory when a durable, audited, replayed, reproduced, or cached result depends on time, randomness, versions, external outcomes, or execution order, or when a deterministic or stochastic contract is claimed. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-record-effective-inputs) · ★1)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★8.2k · 2.4k installs · 28 copies)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if the requirement had been a foundational assumption from day one, instead of bolting it on. - **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when a requirement breaks a foundational assumption and needs coherent redesign. ([jaydubya818/skillz](https://github.com/jaydubya818/skillz/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★1)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Rework the design under the assumption that the requirement existed on day one. ([hellsan631/skills](https://github.com/hellsan631/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principles/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: When you integrate a new requirement into an existing design, redesign as if the requirement had been a foundational assumption from the start instead of bolting it on. ([kpeez/agent-toolbox](https://github.com/kpeez/agent-toolbox/tree/HEAD/plugins/swe/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Reshape the system as if the requirement had always been part of it. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · 2 copies)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Design the shape you would choose if the requirement had existed from day one, then migrate toward that shape. Use when when integrating a new requirement into a design that keeps fighting it. ([TheOnlyFusionCube/potetos-for-everyone](https://github.com/TheOnlyFusionCube/potetos-for-everyone/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-refactoring**: Refactoring discipline — Fowler's catalog of behavior-preserving moves (Extract Function, Inline Variable, Move Function, Replace Conditional with Polymorphism, Introduce Parameter Object, Extract Class), the rule of three, characterization-tests-before-touching-legacy, and small-steps-with-green-between discipline. Distinct from principle-clean-code (naming/length aesthetics) and principle-design-patterns (GoF catalog selection). Auto-load when discussing refactoring strategy, code smells, characterization tests for legacy code, or behavior-preserving structural change. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-refactoring) · ★2)
- **principle-release-engineering**: Release engineering — semver discipline, rollout strategy, rollback planning, expand-contract for breaking changes, kill-switch, idempotent release automation, post-release verification. Auto-load when cutting a release, planning a breaking API change, writing release notes, choosing a version bump, using a feature flag as a kill-switch, or building release automation scripts. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-release-engineering) · ★2)
- **principle-report-the-disqualifier**: Apply when writing or gating any report that makes a causal claim or summarises a cumulative outcome — a backtest, an experiment write-up, a capacity or cost model, a postmortem, an eval summary, a benchmark table. The report must carry what would change the reader's decision: what observation would refute the claim, whether the path crossed a state it could not return from, what the headline was before and after the fix, and which unvaried assumption flips the sign. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-report-the-disqualifier) · ★2)
- **principle-resiliency**: Resiliency principles — fault tolerance, partial failure, failure domains, bulkheads, fail-fast vs fail-soft, health checks, liveness vs readiness probes, idempotency keys, safe retry, deduplication, exactly-once effects, token and leaky buckets, synchronized retries, and 429 responses. Auto-load when isolating dependencies with bulkheads, choosing fail-fast vs fail-soft, configuring health/readiness/liveness probes, making operations safely retryable with idempotency keys or dedup stores, preventing double execution, or adding jitter to prevent thundering-herd retry spikes. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-resiliency) · ★2)
- **principle-respect-the-shared-runtime**: Apply to any Apex transaction design. Governor limits are uncatchable: a breach kills the transaction with System.LimitException and no try/catch recovers it. Design so limits are never approached; probe headroom with the Limits class. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-respect-the-shared-runtime))
- **principle-respect-transaction-boundaries**: Limit atomicity and isolation claims to resources in one enforced commit boundary. Mandatory when several changes or effects are claimed to commit or roll back together, a read-then-write invariant depends on isolation, or state and outbound intent are settled together. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-respect-transaction-boundaries) · ★1)
- **principle-scope-the-session**: Apply when a session's actual work drifts away from what it was opened to do — a side-investigation becomes the main thread, or an unrelated request gets absorbed into an existing session. Start a fresh session instead of letting scope creep silently expand the one you're in. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-scope-the-session) · ★2)
- **principle-security**: Security design principles — trust boundaries and input validation, secure defaults and defense in depth, lightweight threat modeling, cryptography hygiene, RBAC/ABAC authorization models, OAuth 2.0/OIDC token validation. Auto-load when designing auth, discussing authn or authz, handling secrets or credentials, defining trust boundaries, validating untrusted input, considering SSRF or CSRF, configuring TLS, weighing least-privilege trade-offs, choosing RBAC or ABAC, handling PII or GDPR data, or pinning deps and generating an SBOM. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-security) · ★2)
- **principle-selective-queries**: Apply when writing or reviewing SOQL or SOSL. Every query filters on indexed fields with positive operators, returns only the fields needed, and stays selective at production volume: no leading-wildcard LIKE, no negative operators, no formula fields in WHERE without a custom index. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-selective-queries))
- **principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**: Apply when concurrent actors might write to the same file, branch, key, or state object. Eliminate the sharing first; serialize structurally only when one shared writer is a real invariant. - **principle-sequence-verifiable-units**: Apply to multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and to how you stack commits and PRs. - **principle-solid**: SOLID principles — SRP, OCP, LSP, ISP, DIP (single responsibility, open-closed, Liskov substitution, interface segregation, dependency inversion). ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-solid) · ★2)
- **principle-sql-preserve-query-cardinality**: Preserve root selection, child completeness, ordering and bounded query work when implementing or reviewing SQL aggregate reads, filters, joins or pagination. - **principle-sql-respect-transaction-ownership**: Preserve the caller's atomic operation when implementing or reviewing SQL transaction composition, participating readers and writers, or rollback behavior. - **principle-sql-use-jsonb-deliberately**: Establish a versioned, queryable storage contract when introducing or changing meaningful JSONB payloads, their readers, migrations or external query projections. - **principle-state-consistency-contracts**: State the consistency, freshness, ordering, authority, and fallback required by each read or decision. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-state-consistency-contracts) · ★1)
- **principle-structure-by-ownership**: Apply when placing code, shaping monorepo packages, or reorganizing feature folders and imports. - **principle-style-serves-outcomes**: Apply when a style, idiom, readability, or abstraction choice could change runtime cost. ([taekwondodev/skills](https://github.com/taekwondodev/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-style-serves-outcomes))
- **principle-subagent-inherits-scope**: Apply when spawning or prompting a subagent, fork, explorer, investigator, judge, or worker. A subagent may not act outside the scope its parent gave it, and may not widen that scope on its own. A subagent that finds the parent's premise wrong surfaces the contradiction with evidence instead of acting on it or quietly conforming to it. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-subagent-inherits-scope) · ★2)
- **principle-subtract-before-you-add**: Apply when sequencing an addition, refactor, or rewrite. Remove dead code, redundant validators, and stub references first, then build on the simpler base. - **principle-tdd**: Test-Driven Development (TDD) — red-green-refactor, spec first, Arrange-Act-Assert; making tests fast and isolated; test doubles (mock, stub, fake, spy), mocking-as-design-feedback, outside-in vs inside-out TDD. - **principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**: Apply when you write, change, or keep a test. Assert what the code's users can observe, so that the test fails when that behavior breaks and keeps passing when only the internals change. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation) · ★8.2k · 22 copies)
- **principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**: Apply when you write, change, or keep a test. Identify a relevant defect and check that the test detects it. Assert the required result or observable effect, including absence when the contract requires it. ([michael-denyer/pstack-claude](https://github.com/michael-denyer/pstack-claude/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation) · ★497 · 2 copies)
- **principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**: Apply when you write, change, or keep a test. Call the code the way its users do and assert the observable result or contract. Check whether a relevant defect makes the test fail. Keep useful negative-path and relational contract tests. ([ericlitman/open-pstack](https://github.com/ericlitman/open-pstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation) · ★286 · 2 copies)
- **principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**: Apply when you write, change, or keep a test. Call the code the way its users do and assert the result they observe against a literal expected value. ([Aqua-123/pstack-for-codex](https://github.com/Aqua-123/pstack-for-codex/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation) · ★70 · 2 copies)
- **principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**: Apply when you write, change, or keep a test. Call the code the way its users do and assert the result they observe against a literal expected value. If the test would still pass when every imported function returns undefined, rewrite the assertion or delete the test. ([adriannoes/awesome-agentic-ai](https://github.com/adriannoes/awesome-agentic-ai/tree/HEAD/cursor-claude-codex/skills/pstack/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation) · ★60 · 32 copies)
- **principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**: Apply when you write, change, or keep a test. Assert observable behavior so the test catches a broken contract and survives internal refactors. ([fullerzz/agent-skills](https://github.com/fullerzz/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation) · ★2)
- **principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**: Apply when tests mirror internals instead of asserting observable outcomes. ([jaydubya818/skillz](https://github.com/jaydubya818/skillz/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation) · ★1)
- **principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**: Apply when writing or reviewing tests. Verify observable outcomes and identify assertions that cannot detect a relevant defect. ([nahuelb/pstack-codex](https://github.com/nahuelb/pstack-codex/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation) · ★1)
- **principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**: Apply when you write, change, or keep a test. Call the code the way its users do and assert an observable result or effect against an independent expectation. Judge the test by the regression it catches, not its matcher names. ([DBULL7/pstack-pi](https://github.com/DBULL7/pstack-pi/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation))
- **principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**: When you write, change, or keep a test, assert what the code's users can observe, so that the test fails when that behavior breaks and keeps passing when only the internals change. ([kpeez/agent-toolbox](https://github.com/kpeez/agent-toolbox/tree/HEAD/plugins/swe/skills/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation))

If the test would still pass when the code does nothing, rewrite the assertion or delete the test. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation))

([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation) · 2 copies)
- **principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**: Apply when writing, changing or keeping any test. ([MVPavan/coding-ritual](https://github.com/MVPavan/coding-ritual/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation))
- **principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**: Apply when you write, change, or keep a test. Call the code as its users do and assert the exact result they see. - **principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**: Choose and review tests that protect observable behavior and survive internal refactoring. Use when writing regression tests, assessing test quality, or deciding whether coverage proves a change. ([ossianhempel/ossian-stack](https://github.com/ossianhempel/ossian-stack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation))
- **principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**: Call the system the way its consumer does and assert meaningful observable output; avoid mocks or assertions that only mirror internal calls. Use when when writing or evaluating tests. ([TheOnlyFusionCube/potetos-for-everyone](https://github.com/TheOnlyFusionCube/potetos-for-everyone/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation))
- **principle-test-boundaries**: Choose the narrowest test that preserves the dependency semantics under claim. Mandatory when selecting a direct, boundary, integration, or end-to-end test, or choosing a test double or real dependency. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/testing/principle-test-boundaries) · ★1)
- **principle-test-characterization**: Record current observable behavior before changing its implementation. Mandatory when refactoring behavior whose contract is missing, disputed, or weakly tested. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/testing/principle-test-characterization) · ★1)
- **principle-test-determinism**: Control time, randomness, scheduling, retries, cancellation, and concurrency in tests. Mandatory when those inputs can change the result or timing of a test. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/testing/principle-test-determinism) · ★1)
- **principle-test-execution**: Prove the intended tests actually ran. Mandatory whenever a test command is cited as evidence or execution can be skipped, filtered, cached, environment-gated, dependency-gated, or split across runners.
